Currently, two official plugins are available: - [@vitejs/plugin-react](https://github.com/vitejs/vite-plugin-react/blob/main/packages/plugin-react) uses [Babel](https://babeljs.io/) (or [oxc](https://oxc.rs) when used in [rolldown-vite](https://vite.dev/guide/rolldown)) for Fast Refresh - [@vitejs/plugin-react-swc](https://github.com/vitejs/vite-plugin-react/blob/main/packages/plugin-react-swc) uses [SWC](https://swc.rs/) for Fast Refresh ## React Compiler The React Compiler is not enabled on this template because of its impact on dev & build performances. To add it, see [this documentation](https://react.dev/learn/react-compiler/installation). ## Expanding the ESLint configuration If you are developing a production application, we recommend updating the configuration to enable type-aware lint rules: ```js export default defineConfig([ globalIgnores(['dist']), { files: ['**/*.{ts,tsx}'], extends: [ // Other configs... // Remove tseslint.configs.recommended and replace with this tseslint.configs.recommendedTypeChecked, // Alternatively, use this for stricter rules tseslint.configs.strictTypeChecked, // Optionally, add this for stylistic rules tseslint.configs.stylisticTypeChecked, // Other configs... ], languageOptions: { parserOptions: { project: ['./tsconfig.node.json', './tsconfig.app.json'], tsconfigRootDir: import.meta.dirname, }, // other options... }, }, ]) ``` You can also install [eslint-plugin-react-x](https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/tree/main/packages/plugins/eslint-plugin-react-x) and [eslint-plugin-react-dom](https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/tree/main/packages/plugins/eslint-plugin-react-dom) for React-specific lint rules: ```js // eslint.config.js import reactX from 'eslint-plugin-react-x' import reactDom from 'eslint-plugin-react-dom' export default defineConfig([ globalIgnores(['dist']), { files: ['**/*.{ts,tsx}'], extends: [ // Other configs... // Enable lint rules for React reactX.configs['recommended-typescript'], // Enable lint rules for React DOM reactDom.configs.recommended, ], languageOptions: { parserOptions: { project: ['./tsconfig.node.json', './tsconfig.app.json'], tsconfigRootDir: import.meta.dirname, }, // other options... }, }, ]) ``` ================================================================================ FILE: api/auth-router.ts ================================================================================ import * as cookie from "cookie"; import { Session } from "@contracts/constants"; import { getSessionCookieOptions } from "./lib/cookies"; import { createRouter, authedQuery } from "./middleware"; export const authRouter = createRouter({ me: authedQuery.query((opts) => opts.ctx.user), logout: authedQuery.mutation(async ({ ctx }) => { const opts = getSessionCookieOptions(ctx.req.headers); ctx.resHeaders.append( "set-cookie", cookie.serialize(Session.cookieName, "", { httpOnly: opts.httpOnly, path: opts.path, sameSite: opts.sameSite?.toLowerCase() as "lax" | "none", secure: opts.secure, maxAge: 0, }), ); return { success: true }; }), }); ================================================================================ FILE: api/boot.ts ================================================================================ import { Hono } from "hono"; import { bodyLimit } from "hono/body-limit"; import type { HttpBindings } from "@hono/node-server"; import { fetchRequestHandler } from "@trpc/server/adapters/fetch"; import { appRouter } from "./router"; import { createContext } from "./context"; import { env } from "./lib/env"; import { createOAuthCallbackHandler } from "./kimi/auth"; import { Paths } from "@contracts/constants"; import { log } from "./lib/logger"; import { handleClientLog } from "./lib/clientlog"; import { REQUEST_ID_HEADER, runWithRequestId } from "./lib/requestcontext"; import { newsRoutes } from "./routes/news"; const app = new Hono<{ Bindings: HttpBindings }>(); // One middleware for both halves of request observability, because they need the // same scope and the same id: // - G3 correlation: mint a request id, echo it on the response header (what the // browser logger reads), and run the rest of the request inside it so every // server log line carries it. // - G4 access log: one line per request with method/path/status/duration, so // "who called what and how long did it take" is answerable. Until now only // failures were logged at all, and success left no trace. // /api/log is skipped: it is the browser's log sink, and an access line per // POSTed batch would be self-amplifying noise in the very file it writes. app.use("*", async (c, next) => { const requestId = crypto.randomUUID(); if (c.req.path === "/api/log") { await next(); return; } const start = Date.now(); await runWithRequestId(requestId, async () => { await next(); // Set the header AFTER next(): the tRPC route hands Hono a raw Response // built by fetchRequestHandler, which carries its own headers, so a // c.header() called before dispatch is dropped on that path. Setting it on // the resolved c.res covers every route, including the tRPC one. c.res.headers.set(REQUEST_ID_HEADER, requestId); // Only /api/* is logged. In production serveStaticFiles() registers a // catch-all AFTER this middleware, so a page load pulls in every asset as // its own request — logging those would bury the API calls the agent reads // this file for, and inflate the log for no diagnostic gain. if (!c.req.path.startsWith("/api/")) return; log.info("request", { method: c.req.method, path: c.req.path, status: c.res.status, durationMs: Date.now() - start, }); }); }); // Scoped to the tRPC route, NOT global: on the dev server (@hono/vite-dev-server) // a global bodyLimit breaks bodyless requests. Hono's bodyLimit reads any truthy // request stream to the end and then rebuilds the Request with `new Request(raw, // { body, duplex: "half" })`; the dev server's Response/Request class makes that // rebuild throw `TypeError: Cannot read private member #state ...`, so every // bodyless request (DELETE/PATCH on the app's own REST routes) 500s at dispatch. // /api/log keeps its own 64KB caps in lib/clientlog.ts, so it needs no middleware. app.use("/api/trpc/*", bodyLimit({ maxSize: 50 * 1024 * 1024 })); app.get(Paths.oauthCallback, createOAuthCallbackHandler()); app.use("/api/trpc/*", async (c) => { return fetchRequestHandler({ endpoint: "/api/trpc", req: c.req.raw, router: appRouter, createContext, }); }); // Browser-side log sink (see lib/clientlog.ts). Must be registered before the // /api/* 404 catch-all below. bodyLimit guards it too: lib/clientlog.ts caps the // body itself, but only AFTER Hono has read it into memory, and this route sits // on the public preview URL — the cap has to bite before the read. app.post( "/api/log", bodyLimit({ maxSize: 64 * 1024, onError: (c) => c.body(null, 413) }), handleClientLog, ); app.route("/api/news", newsRoutes); app.all("/api/*", (c) => c.json({ error: "Not Found" }, 404)); // Unhandled request errors must reach /tmp/kimi-logs/app.log, not just stdout: // that file is what the building agent reads when a preview 500s, and without // this the log looks clean while the request actually failed. Response stays a // generic 500 (no stack leaked to the caller); the detail goes to the log only. app.onError((err, c) => { log.error("unhandled request error", { method: c.req.method, path: c.req.path, error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err), stack: err instanceof Error ? err.stack : undefined, }); return c.json({ error: "Internal Server Error" }, 500); }); export default app; if (env.isProduction) { const { serve } = await import("@hono/node-server"); const { serveStaticFiles } = await import("./lib/vite"); serveStaticFiles(app); const port = parseInt(process.env.PORT || "3000"); serve({ fetch: app.fetch, port }, () => { log.info(`Server running on http://localhost:${port}/`); }); } ================================================================================ FILE: api/context.ts ================================================================================ import type { FetchCreateContextFnOptions } from "@trpc/server/adapters/fetch"; import type { User } from "@db/schema"; import { authenticateRequest } from "./kimi/auth"; export type TrpcContext = { req: Request; resHeaders: Headers; user?: User; }; export async function createContext( opts: FetchCreateContextFnOptions, ): Promise { const ctx: TrpcContext = { req: opts.req, resHeaders: opts.resHeaders }; try { ctx.user = await authenticateRequest(opts.req.headers); } catch { // Authentication is optional here } return ctx; } ================================================================================ FILE: api/feeds.ts ================================================================================ FILE: api/kimi/auth.ts ================================================================================ import type { Context } from "hono"; import { setCookie } from "hono/cookie"; import * as jose from "jose"; import * as cookie from "cookie"; import { env } from "../lib/env"; import { getSessionCookieOptions } from "../lib/cookies"; import { Session } from "@contracts/constants"; import { Errors } from "@contracts/errors"; import { signSessionToken, verifySessionToken } from "./session"; import { users as kimiUsers } from "./platform"; import { findUserByUnionId, upsertUser } from "../queries/users"; import type { TokenResponse } from "./types"; async function exchangeAuthCode( code: string, redirectUri: string, ): Promise { const body = new URLSearchParams({ grant_type: "authorization_code", code, client_id: env.appId, redirect_uri: redirectUri, client_secret: env.appSecret, }); const resp = await fetch(`${env.kimiAuthUrl}/api/oauth/token`, { method: "POST", headers: { "Content-Type": "application/x-www-form-urlencoded" }, body: body.toString(), }); if (!resp.ok) { const text = await resp.text(); throw new Error(`Token exchange failed (${resp.status}): ${text}`); } return resp.json() as Promise; } const jwks = jose.createRemoteJWKSet( new URL(`${env.kimiAuthUrl}/api/.well-known/jwks.json`), ); async function verifyAccessToken( accessToken: string, ): Promise<{ userId: string; clientId: string }> { const { payload } = await { userId, clientId }; } export async function authenticateRequest(headers: Headers) { const cookies = cookie.parse(headers.get("cookie") || ""); const token = cookies[Session.cookieName]; if (!token) { console.warn("[auth] No session cookie found in request."); throw Errors.forbidden("Invalid authentication token."); } const claim = await verifySessionToken(token); if (!claim) { throw Errors.forbidden("Invalid authentication token."); } const user = await findUserByUnionId(claim.unionId); if (!user) { throw Errors.forbidden("User not found. Please re-login."); } return user; } export function createOAuthCallbackHandler() { return async (c: Context) => { const code = c.req.query("code"); const state = c.req.query("state"); const error = c.req.query("error"); const errorDescription = c.req.query("error_description"); if (error) { if (error === "access_denied") { return c.redirect("/", 302); } return c.json( { error, error_description: errorDescription }, 400, ); return c.redirect("/", 302); } catch (error) { console.error("[OAuth] Callback failed", error); return c.json({ error: "OAuth callback failed" }, 500); } }; } export { exchangeAuthCode, verifyAccessToken }; ================================================================================ FILE: api/kimi/session.ts ================================================================================ import * as jose from "jose"; import { env } from "../lib/env"; import type { SessionPayload } from "./types"; const JWT_ALG = "HS256"; export async function signSessionToken( payload: SessionPayload, ): Promise { const secret = new TextEncoder().encode(env.appSecret); return new jose.SignJWT(payload) .setProtectedHeader({ alg: JWT_ALG }) .setIssuedAt() .setExpirationTime("1 year") .sign(secret); } export async function verifySessionToken( token: string, ): Promise { if (!token) { console.warn("[session] No token provided for verification."); return null; } try { const secret = new TextEncoder().encode(env.appSecret); const { payload } = await jose.jwtVerify(token, secret, { algorithms: [JWT_ALG], }); const { unionId, clientId } = payload; if (!unionId || !clientId) { console.warn("[session] JWT payload missing required fields."); return null; } return { unionId, clientId } as SessionPayload; } catch (error) { console.warn("[session] JWT verification failed:", error); return null; } } ================================================================================ FILE: api/kimi/types.ts ================================================================================ export type TokenResponse = { access_token: string; token_type: string; expires_in: number; refresh_token?: string; scope: string; }; export type SessionPayload = { unionId: string; clientId: string; }; export type UserProfile = { user_id: string; name: string; avatar_url: string; }; ================================================================================ FILE: api/lib/clientlog.ts ================================================================================ // Client-log sink: the browser half of the unified log. // // The frontend has no filesystem access to the sandbox, so a browser-side // console.log is invisible to the building agent (it only reads the sandbox). // This endpoint closes that gap: the injected frontend logger POSTs here, and // we append the lines to the same /tmp/kimi-logs/app.log the backend writes — // one file, one format, one read for the agent. // // Guardrails (the preview URL is public — anyone holding it can reach this // route, so treat every field as untrusted): // - cap each field (LOG_VALUE_MAX, applied before serialization so the line // stays valid JSON) and each request body (LOG_BODY_MAX); // - cap writes per window so a hostile caller cannot fill the disk: a // per-identity cap plus a global ceiling, because the identity comes from a // client-supplied header and is therefore rotatable; // - never echo anything back (a log endpoint is not a reflection surface). import type { Context } from "hono"; import { appendLine } from "./logger"; import { scrubCtx, scrubValue } from "./scrub"; const LOG_BODY_MAX = 64 * 1024; const LOG_MAX_LINES_PER_REQUEST = 50; // Token-bucket-ish guard: at most this many lines accepted per client window. const LOG_MAX_LINES_PER_MINUTE = 600; // Global budget: the per-key limit above is keyed on a client-supplied header // (see clientKey), so a caller rotating X-Forwarded-For would otherwise get a // fresh window per request and the cap would never bite. This ceiling is what // actually makes "a hostile caller cannot fill the disk" true; it is reset // every window regardless of how many distinct keys appear. const LOG_MAX_LINES_PER_MINUTE_GLOBAL = 3000; // Upper bound on tracked keys, so rotating identities cannot grow the map // without limit (an unbounded map here is an OOM on the preview process). const WINDOW_MAX_KEYS = 1024; const WINDOW_MS = 60_000; type ClientLine = { level?: string; msg?: string; ctx?: Record; }; type Window = { at: number; count: number }; const windowStart = new Map(); let globalWindow: Window = { at: 0, count: 0 }; // evictStale drops windows that have aged out. Called before inserting a new // key so the map stays bounded even when the caller never repeats a key. function evictStale(now: number): void { if (windowStart.size < WINDOW_MAX_KEYS) return; for (const [k, v] of windowStart) { if (now - v.at > WINDOW_MS) windowStart.delete(k); } // Still full of live windows: drop oldest insertions (Map preserves order). while (windowStart.size >= WINDOW_MAX_KEYS) { const oldest = windowStart.keys().next(); if (oldest.done) break; windowStart.delete(oldest.value); } } // underRateLimit charges one line against both the per-key and the global // window. Either one being exhausted rejects the line. function underRateLimit(key: string): boolean { const now = Date.now(); if (now - globalWindow.at > WINDOW_MS) globalWindow = { at: now, count: 0 }; if (globalWindow.count >= LOG_MAX_LINES_PER_MINUTE_GLOBAL) return false; const cur = windowStart.get(key); if (!cur || now - cur.at > WINDOW_MS) { evictStale(now); windowStart.set(key, { at: now, count: 1 }); globalWindow.count += 1; return true; } if (cur.count >= LOG_MAX_LINES_PER_MINUTE) return false; cur.count += 1; globalWindow.count += 1; return true; } // Truncation must happen on VALUES, not on the serialized line. // // The earlier version sliced the finished JSON at a byte limit and appended a // marker. That cuts inside a string literal, so the line stops being // JSON: a long stack (exactly the case worth reading) produced // `Unterminated string in JSON at position N` and every downstream consumer that // parses app.log dropped the line. Budgeting the fields first keeps each line // valid JSON — oversized values are shortened, the record survives. const LOG_VALUE_MAX = 1024; function clampValue(value: unknown): unknown { if (typeof value === "string") { return value.length > LOG_VALUE_MAX ? value.slice(0, LOG_VALUE_MAX) + "…[truncated]" : value; } return value; } // clampRecord shortens every field (and nested ctx field) so the serialized line // fits. It works on the record, never on the encoded form. function clampRecord( record: Record ): Record { const out: Record = {}; for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(record)) { out[k] = clampValue(v); } return out; } // Reserved log fields. Kept in one place so the writer and the filter cannot // drift apart. const RESERVED_KEYS = new Set(["ts", "source", "level", "msg"]); // reservedFiltered returns ctx minus the reserved keys, so spreading it cannot // override the line's own ts/source/level/msg. function reservedFiltered( ctx: Record | undefined ): Record { if (!ctx) return {}; return Object.fromEntries( Object.entries(ctx).filter(([k]) => !RESERVED_KEYS.has(k)) ); } export async function handleClientLog(c: Context): Promise { const declared = Number(c.req.header("content-length") ?? "0"); if (declared > LOG_BODY_MAX) return c.body(null, 413); let payload: unknown; try { const raw = await c.req.text(); if (raw.length > LOG_BODY_MAX) return c.body(null, 413); payload = JSON.parse(raw); } catch { return c.body(null, 400); } const incoming: ClientLine[] = Array.isArray(payload) ? (payload as ClientLine[]) : [payload as ClientLine]; if (incoming.length > LOG_MAX_LINES_PER_REQUEST) { incoming.length = LOG_MAX_LINES_PER_REQUEST; } // Identity for the rate limit: cf-connecting-ip first, then the forwarded // chain. Absent headers collapse to "anon" — a weak key, but the line cap // still bounds damage. const clientKey = c.req.header("cf-connecting-ip") ?? (c.req.header("x-forwarded-for") ?? "anon").split(",")[0].trim(); for (const item of incoming) { if (!item || typeof item !== "object") continue; if (!underRateLimit(clientKey)) break; const level = typeof item.level === "string" ? item.level.slice(0, 16) : "info"; const msg = typeof item.msg === "string" ? item.msg : ""; const ctx = scrubCtx(item.ctx); // Reserved keys (ts/source/level/msg) are written first and filtered out of // ctx: a caller passing { level: "info" } or { source: "server" } must not // be able to relabel the line or forge its origin — the keyword map in the // building skill keys off `source`. const line = JSON.stringify( clampRecord({ ts: new Date().toISOString(), source: "client", level, msg: scrubValue(msg), ...reservedFiltered(ctx), }) ); appendLine(line); } // No body: this is a sink, not a resource. return c.body(null, 204); } ================================================================================ FILE: api/lib/cookies.ts ================================================================================ import type { CookieOptions } from "hono/utils/cookie"; function isLocalhost(headers: Headers): boolean { const host = headers.get("host") || ""; return host.startsWith("localhost:") || host.startsWith("127.0.0.1:"); } export function getSessionCookieOptions(headers: Headers): CookieOptions { const localhost = isLocalhost(headers); return { httpOnly: true, path: "/", sameSite: localhost ? "Lax" : "None", secure: !localhost, }; } ================================================================================ FILE: api/lib/env.ts ================================================================================ import { oauthEndpoints } from "./oauth-env"; import "dotenv/config"; function required(name: string): string { const value = process.env[name]; if (!value && process.env.NODE_ENV === "production") { throw new Error(`Missing required environment variable: ${name}`); } return value ?? ""; } export const env = { appId: required("APP_ID"), appSecret: required("APP_SECRET"), isProduction: process.env.NODE_ENV === "production", databaseUrl: required("DATABASE_URL"), kimiAuthUrl: oauthEndpoints.KIMI_AUTH_URL ?? required("KIMI_AUTH_URL"), kimiOpenUrl: oauthEndpoints.KIMI_OPEN_URL ?? required("KIMI_OPEN_URL"), ownerUnionId: process.env.OWNER_UNION_ID ?? "", }; ================================================================================ FILE: api/lib/http.ts ================================================================================ interface RequestConfig extends RequestInit { baseUrl?: string; params?: Record; timeout?: number; } export class HttpClient { private baseUrl: string; private defaultHeaders: Record; constructor(baseURL: string, opts?: { headers?: Record }) { this.baseUrl = baseURL; this.defaultHeaders = { "Content-Type": "application/json", ...opts?.headers, }; } async request(endpoint: string, config: RequestConfig = {}): Promise { const { method = "GET", params, body, headers, timeout = 30000, ...rest } = config; const url = new URL(`${this.baseUrl}${endpoint}`); if (params) { Object.entries(params).forEach(([key, value]) => url.searchParams.append(key, value.toString()), ); } const controller = new AbortController(); const timeoutId = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), timeout); try { const response = await fetch(url.toString(), { ...rest, method, headers: { ...this.defaultHeaders, ...headers }, body: body ? JSON.stringify(body) : undefined, signal: controller.signal, }); clearTimeout(timeoutId); if (!response.ok) { const errorData = (await response .json() .catch(() => ({}))) as Record; throw new Error(errorData.message || `HTTP Error: ${response.status}`); } return (await response.json()) as T; } catch (error) { // Narrow without `any`: an AbortError from our own controller means the // timeout fired, which is worth a clearer message than the raw DOMException. if (error instanceof Error && error.name === "AbortError") { throw new Error("Request timeout"); } throw error; } } get( url: string, params?: RequestConfig["params"], config?: RequestConfig, ) { // Spread `config` FIRST: the helper's own method/params must win over // anything in the caller's config, but its `timeout`/`headers`/`signal` // must still reach request() — passing config as a second positional // argument used to be silent: the timeout the caller set was dropped and // the request ran on the 30s default. return this.request(url, { ...config, method: "GET", params }); } post(url: string, body?: unknown, config?: RequestConfig) { // Cast at this boundary: request()'s `body` is BodyInit|undefined. Don't // spell it `BodyInit` — tsconfig.server.json is lib:[ES2022] + @types/node, // where that name does not exist (TS2304); read it off RequestInit instead. return this.request(url, { ...config, method: "POST", body: body as RequestInit["body"], }); } } ================================================================================ FILE: api/lib/logger.ts ================================================================================ // Structured logger for the backend, mirrored to /tmp/kimi-logs/app.log. // // Why a file at all: the sandbox-local log is what the building agent reads to // debug a preview that will not start or a request that 500s. stdout is // collected by the platform's log pipeline, but the agent inspects the sandbox // directly (its shell and this process share one sandbox), so the two sinks // are written from one call — same bytes in both. // // Zero dependencies on purpose: this file is grafted into every user project, // so pulling in pino/winston would add weight to the template's dependency // surface for a feature whose whole job is "append one JSON line". import fs from "fs"; import path from "path"; import { currentRequestId } from "./requestcontext"; import { scrubCtx, scrubValue } from "./scrub"; // Fixed path (not PID-suffixed): the agent must be able to name it without // discovering a pid. /tmp is sandbox-local: it dies with the box, which is // intended (this is preview-time debugging, not post-mortem forensics) and it // never lands in the user's workspace (that tree is git-archived and shipped at // publish time). // // It is NOT "naturally bounded" — that was wishful. The rate limits in // clientlog.ts cap the *browser* path per minute, but server lines, access-log // lines and (in production) static-asset requests are not rate limited at all, // so a busy preview can write gigabytes over a long session and fill the disk, // turning a logging feature into a new outage class. Hence an explicit byte // budget below: past it the file is truncated once, with a marker, so the newest // evidence survives and the total stays bounded. const LOG_DIR = process.env.KIMI_LOG_DIR ?? "/tmp/kimi-logs"; const LOG_FILE = process.env.KIMI_LOG_FILE ?? path.join(LOG_DIR, "app.log"); // 8 MiB is far more than a debugging session reads and small enough that even a // hostile write rate cannot exhaust a sandbox disk. const LOG_MAX_BYTES = 8 * 1024 * 1024; let bytesWritten = 0; let sizeKnown = false; // enforceBudget truncates the file once it exceeds LOG_MAX_BYTES. Doing it here // (on the write path) rather than with a timer keeps the bound independent of // whether anything else in the process is still alive — the failure mode being // guarded is a runaway writer, which by definition keeps calling in. function enforceBudget(lineLength: number): void { if (!sizeKnown) { try { bytesWritten = fs.statSync(LOG_FILE).size; } catch { bytesWritten = 0; } sizeKnown = true; } if (bytesWritten + lineLength <= LOG_MAX_BYTES) return; try { fs.writeFileSync( LOG_FILE, JSON.stringify({ ts: new Date().toISOString(), level: "warn", msg: "app.log exceeded its size budget; truncated", maxBytes: LOG_MAX_BYTES, }) + "\n" ); } catch { // If we cannot truncate, keep appending: losing old lines is better than // losing the process's ability to log at all. return; } bytesWritten = 0; } let dirReady = false; function ensureDir(): void { if (dirReady) return; try { fs.mkdirSync(LOG_DIR, { recursive: true }); dirReady = true; } catch { // A read-only or missing /tmp must not crash the app; stdout still works. } } type Level = "debug" | "info" | "warn" | "error"; // Reserved fields the caller must not be able to override from ctx — same gate // clientlog.ts applies to browser-supplied context. Without it, a ctx carrying // `level`/`msg`/`ts` would rewrite the line's own identity (and could relabel a // `warn` as `error`, or forge a timestamp). const RESERVED_KEYS = new Set(["ts", "level", "msg", "requestId"]); function write(level: Level, msg: string, ctx?: Record): void { // Serialization is inside the try: a cyclic or BigInt-bearing ctx throws, and // logging must never take a request down with it (the file sink below is // explicitly best-effort for the same reason). let line: string; try { // The request id is added here rather than at each call site: app code keeps // calling log.error(msg, ctx) and the correlation comes for free. It is // spread FIRST so an explicit ctx.requestId still wins. const requestId = currentRequestId(); const extra = Object.fromEntries( Object.entries(scrubCtx(ctx) ?? {}).filter(([k]) => !RESERVED_KEYS.has(k)) ); line = JSON.stringify({ ts: new Date().toISOString(), level, ...(requestId ? { requestId } : {}), // The message is scrubbed too: secrets frequently arrive inside it (a URL // with a query token), not only in ctx. msg: scrubValue(msg), ...extra, }); } catch { return; } // stdout first: if the file append throws, the message is not lost. if (level === "error") console.error(line); else if (level === "warn") console.warn(line); // debug and info both go to stdout via console.log: the sandbox file sink is // what distinguishes them (the `level` field), and console.debug is a no-op // under some defaults, which would drop the line from stdout too. else console.log(line); ensureDir(); try { enforceBudget(line.length + 1); fs.appendFileSync(LOG_FILE, line + "\n"); bytesWritten += line.length + 1; } catch { // Best-effort sink; never let logging break a request. } } // appendLine is the sink the client-log endpoint writes through, so browser // logs land in exactly the same file with the same shape as server logs. // // It also echoes to stdout, for the same reason write() does: the platform log // pipeline collects stdout, so a file-only browser line vanished the moment the // sandbox was recycled. Server lines already had both sinks; browser lines // having only the file was an inconsistency, not a design choice. export function appendLine(line: string): void { try { console.log(line); } catch { // stdout is best-effort too. } ensureDir(); try { enforceBudget(line.length + 1); fs.appendFileSync(LOG_FILE, line + "\n"); bytesWritten += line.length + 1; } catch { // Best-effort. } } export const log = { info: (msg: string, ctx?: Record) => write("info", msg, ctx), warn: (msg: string, ctx?: Record) => write("warn", msg, ctx), error: (msg: string, ctx?: Record) => write("error", msg, ctx), // debug exists so app code has a file-backed level below info. Without it the // natural fallback is console.debug, which the sandbox file sink never sees — // so "verbose logging in the app" would silently be invisible to the agent. debug: (msg: string, ctx?: Record) => write("debug", msg, ctx), }; export const logFilePath = LOG_FILE; ================================================================================ FILE: api/lib/oauth-env.ts ================================================================================ import { readFileSync } from "node:fs"; import { parse } from "dotenv"; // Only the OAuth pair is artifact-owned. APP_ID/APP_SECRET, DB and user-secret // precedence stays unchanged; env-less/partial legacy files keep platform defaults. let local: Record = {}; try { local = parse(readFileSync(".env")); } catch (error) { if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "ENOENT") throw error; } export const oauthEndpoints: Record = local.KIMI_AUTH_URL && local.KIMI_OPEN_URL ? { KIMI_AUTH_URL: local.KIMI_AUTH_URL, KIMI_OPEN_URL: local.KIMI_OPEN_URL } : {}; ================================================================================ FILE: api/lib/requestcontext.ts ================================================================================ // Per-request correlation for the sandbox log. // // Why this exists: app.log holds BOTH halves of a request — the server's // `log.error(...)` and the browser's POSTed `log.error(...)` — and without a // shared key the two lines cannot be lined up. The agent debugging a 500 has // the server stack but cannot tell which browser error belongs to it. // // How the id travels: the server mints it once per inbound request (a plain // random UUID — there is no distributed trace here, just a correlation key), // echoes it on the `X-Request-Id` response header, and stamps it on every server // log line written while that request is in flight. The browser reads the header // and attaches the same id to the lines it POSTs to /api/log. One request, one // id, two sinks. // // Implementation note: the id lives in an AsyncLocalStorage rather than in the // log call's own argument, so app code keeps calling `log.error(msg, ctx)` and // the correlation is added for free. Node's ALS is the right primitive here — // it survives `await` boundaries, so a handler that logs after an await still // carries the id of the request that started it. import { AsyncLocalStorage } from "async_hooks"; export const REQUEST_ID_HEADER = "x-request-id"; const storage = new AsyncLocalStorage(); // runWithRequestId scopes `fn` to a request id. The Hono middleware wraps every // request in this, so anything logged inside — including after awaits — picks // the id up via currentRequestId(). export function runWithRequestId(requestId: string, fn: () => T): T { return storage.run(requestId, fn); } // currentRequestId returns the id of the request in flight, or undefined outside // any request scope (startup logging, a timer callback, a client-log write). export function currentRequestId(): string | undefined { return storage.getStore(); } ================================================================================ FILE: api/lib/scrub.ts ================================================================================ // Secret scrubbing, shared by both log sinks. // // It lives in its own module because BOTH halves of app.log need it: the browser // sink (clientlog.ts) and the server logger (logger.ts). The browser side has // always scrubbed, so a doc claim like "secrets are redacted before they are // written" held for browser lines and silently did NOT hold for server lines — // the caller most likely to pass a URL with a token is server code // (`log.error(msg, { url })`), so the unprotected half was the one that mattered. // // What it catches: values under a key that names a credential, and // `Bearer …` / `?token=…` inside any string (the leak usually hides in a URL // where the enclosing key is innocuous). Depth is capped so a cyclic object // cannot recurse forever. const SECRET_KEY_RE = /(token|secret|password|passwd|api[-_]?key|authorization|cookie|session)/i; const SECRET_QUERY_RE = /([?&](?:[^=&#\s]*?(?:token|secret|password|key|kpt|auth|session)[^=&#\s]*)=)[^&#\s]+/gi; const BEARER_RE = /(Bearer\s+)[A-Za-z0-9._~+/=-]+/gi; const SCRUB_MAX_DEPTH = 6; export function scrubValue(value: unknown, depth = 0): unknown { if (typeof value === "string") { return value .replace(BEARER_RE, "$1") .replace(SECRET_QUERY_RE, "$1"); } if (depth >= SCRUB_MAX_DEPTH || !value || typeof value !== "object") { return value; } if (Array.isArray(value)) { return value.map(v => scrubValue(v, depth + 1)); } const out: Record = {}; for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(value as Record)) { // Key match applies at every level, not just the top one. out[k] = SECRET_KEY_RE.test(k) ? "" : scrubValue(v, depth + 1); } return out; } // scrubCtx applies scrubValue and keeps the result an object (the callers spread // it into a log record). A non-object input collapses to undefined. export function scrubCtx( ctx: Record | undefined ): Record | undefined { if (!ctx) return undefined; const scrubbed = scrubValue(ctx, 0); return typeof scrubbed === "object" && scrubbed !== null && !Array.isArray(scrubbed) ? (scrubbed as Record) : undefined; } ================================================================================ FILE: api/lib/vite.ts ================================================================================ import type { Hono } from "hono"; import type { HttpBindings } from "@hono/node-server"; import { serveStatic } from "@hono/node-server/serve-static"; import fs from "fs"; import path from "path"; type App = Hono<{ Bindings: HttpBindings }>; export function serveStaticFiles(app: App) { const distPath = path.resolve(import.meta.dirname, "../dist/public"); app.use("*", serveStatic({ root: "./dist/public" })); app.notFound((c) => { const accept = c.req.header("accept") ?? ""; if (!accept.includes("text/html")) { return c.json({ error: "Not Found" }, 404); } const indexPath = path.resolve(distPath, "index.html"); const content = fs.readFileSync(indexPath, "utf-8"); return c.html(content); }); } ================================================================================ FILE: api/middleware.ts ================================================================================ import { ErrorMessages } from "@contracts/constants"; import { initTRPC, TRPCError } from "@trpc/server"; import superjson from "superjson"; import type { TrpcContext } from "./context"; import { log } from "./lib/logger"; const t = initTRPC.context().create({ transformer: superjson, // G9: a procedure that throws never reaches app.onError — the tRPC fetch // adapter catches it and returns its own 500 JSON, so the Hono error handler // is bypassed entirely. That left the most common real failure (a mutation // 500ing) with NO structured record in app.log on the server side; it only // appeared if the caller happened to log it in its own catch. This hook is the // missing server-side safety net. INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR only: the TRPCErrors // thrown below (UNAUTHORIZED / FORBIDDEN) are deliberate business answers, not // crashes, and logging those would drown the file. errorFormatter({ shape, error }) { if (error.code === "INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR") { log.error("trpc procedure failed", { path: shape.data?.path, code: error.code, error: error.message, stack: error.stack, }); } return shape; }, }); export const createRouter = t.router; export const publicQuery = t.procedure; const requireAuth = t.middleware(async (opts) => { const { ctx, next } = opts; if (!ctx.user) { throw new TRPCError({ code: "UNAUTHORIZED", message: ErrorMessages.unauthenticated, }); } return next({ ctx: { ...ctx, user: ctx.user } }); }); function requireRole(role: string) { return t.middleware(async (opts) => { const { ctx, next } = opts; if (!ctx.user || ctx.user.role !== role) { throw new TRPCError({ code: "FORBIDDEN", message: ErrorMessages.insufficientRole, }); } return next({ ctx: { ...ctx, user: ctx.user } }); }); } export const authedQuery = t.procedure.use(requireAuth); export const adminQuery = authedQuery.use(requireRole("admin")); ================================================================================ FILE: api/newsService.ts ================================================================================ import { XMLParser } from "fast-xml-parser"; import type { NewsItem, NewsResponse, RegionId, SectionId, SourceStatus, } from "@contracts/news"; import { feedsFor, type FeedSource } from "./feeds"; const FEED_TTL = 5 * 60 * 1000; const STALE_TTL = 12 * 60 * 60 * 1000; const FETCH_TIMEOUT = 9_000; const MAX_ITEMS_PER_FEED = 30; const MAX_ITEMS_PER_SECTION = 140; const parser = new XMLParser({ ignoreAttributes: false, attributeNamePrefix: "@_", textNodeName: "#text", parseTagValue: false, parseAttributeValue: false, processEntities: true, }); type XmlRecord = Record; type CachedFeed = { items: NewsItem[]; fetchedAt: number }; type FeedResult = { source: FeedSource; items: NewsItem[]; status: SourceStatus["status"]; fromCache: boolean; message?: string; }; const feedCache = new Map(); const inFlight = new Map>(); function record(value: unknown): XmlRecord | null { return value !== null && typeof value === "object" && !Array.isArray(value) ? (value as XmlRecord) : null; } function list(value: T | T[] | undefined | null): T[] { if (value === undefined || value === null) return []; return Array.isArray(value) ? value : [value]; } function text(value: unknown): string { if (typeof value === "string" || typeof value === "number") return String(value).trim(); if (Array.isArray(value)) return text(value[0]); const node = record(value); return node ? text(node["#text"]) : ""; } function attribute(value: unknown, name: string): string { const node = record(value); return node ? text(node[`@_${name}`]) : ""; } function safeUrl(value: string): string | null { try { const url = new URL(value); return url.protocol === "https:" ? url.toString() : null; } catch { return null; } } function decodeEntities(value: string): string { const named: Record = { amp: "&", lt: "<", gt: ">", quot: '"', apos: "'", nbsp: " ", }; return value.replace(/&(#x?[0-9a-f]+|[a-z]+);/gi, (match, entity: string) => { if (entity[0] !== "#") return named[entity.toLowerCase()] ?? match; const hex = entity[1]?.toLowerCase() === "x"; const code = Number.parseInt(entity.slice(hex ? 2 : 1), hex ? 16 : 10); return Number.isFinite(code) ? String.fromCodePoint(code) : match; }); } function plainText(value: unknown, maxLength = 320): string { const cleaned = decodeEntities(text(value)) .replace(/]*>[\s\S]*?<\/script>/gi, " ") .replace(/]*>[\s\S]*?<\/style>/gi, " ") .replace(/<[^>]+>/g, " ") .replace(/\s+/g, " ") .trim(); return cleaned.length > maxLength ? `${cleaned.slice(0, maxLength - 1).trim()}…` : cleaned; } function atomLink(value: unknown): string { const links = list(value); const preferred = links.find((link) => { const rel = attribute(link, "rel"); return !rel || rel === "alternate"; }); return attribute(preferred ?? links[0], "href"); } function imageFromItem(item: XmlRecord, content: string): string | null { const candidates = [ item["media:thumbnail"], item["media:content"], item["itunes:image"], item.enclosure, ]; for (const candidate of candidates.flatMap(list)) { const type = attribute(candidate, "type"); const medium = attribute(candidate, "medium"); if (type && !type.startsWith("image/") && medium !== "image") continue; const url = safeUrl(attribute(candidate, "url") || attribute(candidate, "href")); if (url) return url; } const match = content.match(/]+src=["']([^"']+)["']/i); return match?.[1] ? safeUrl(match[1]) : null; } function canonicalUrl(value: string): string { try { const url = new URL(value); url.hash = ""; for (const key of [...url.searchParams.keys()]) { if (/^(utm_|fbclid$|gclid$)/i.test(key)) url.searchParams.delete(key); } return url.toString(); } catch { return value; } } function normalizeItem( raw: unknown, source: FeedSource, section: SectionId, atom: boolean, ): NewsItem | null { const item = record(raw); if (!item) return null; const title = plainText(item.title, 240); const url = safeUrl(atom ? atomLink(item.link) : text(item.link)); if (!title || !url) return null; const content = text(item["content:encoded"] ?? item.content ?? item.description ?? item.summary); const published = text(item.pubDate ?? item.published ?? item.updated ?? item["dc:date"]); const date = published ? new Date(published) : null; const itemSection = section === "top" ? source.sections.find((candidate) => candidate !== "top") ?? section : section; return { id: canonicalUrl(url), title, url, summary: plainText(item.description ?? item.summary ?? content), imageUrl: imageFromItem(item, content), sourceId: source.id, sourceName: source.name, publishedAt: date && Number.isFinite(date.getTime()) ? date.toISOString() : null, section: itemSection, region: source.region ?? null, }; } function parseFeed(xml: string, source: FeedSource, section: SectionId): NewsItem[] { const document = parser.parse(xml) as XmlRecord; const rss = record(document.rss); const rdf = record(document["rdf:RDF"]); const atom = record(document.feed); const channel = record(rss?.channel ?? rdf?.channel); const rawItems = atom ? list(atom.entry) : list(channel?.item ?? rdf?.item); return rawItems .slice(0, MAX_ITEMS_PER_FEED) .map((item) => normalizeItem(item, source, section, Boolean(atom))) .filter((item): item is NewsItem => item !== null); } async function requestFeed(source: FeedSource, section: SectionId): Promise { const cacheKey = `${source.id}:${section}`; try { const response = await fetch(source.url, { headers: { Accept: "application/rss+xml, application/atom+xml, application/xml, text/xml;q=0.9, */*;q=0.5", "User-Agent": "NetWatch/2.0 (+news feed reader)", }, redirect: "follow", signal: AbortSignal.timeout(FETCH_TIMEOUT), }); if (!response.ok) throw new Error(`HTTP ${response.status}`); const length = Number(response.headers.get("content-length") ?? 0); if (length > 6_000_000) throw new Error("Feed too large"); const xml = await response.text(); const items = parseFeed(xml, source, section); if (!items.length) throw new Error("No readable articles"); feedCache.set(cacheKey, { items, fetchedAt: Date.now() }); return { source, items, status: "ok", fromCache: false }; } catch { const cached = feedCache.get(cacheKey); if (cached && Date.now() - cached.fetchedAt < STALE_TTL) { return { source, items: cached.items, status: "stale", fromCache: true, message: "Showing the most recent cached items", }; } return { source, items: [], status: "error", fromCache: false, message: "Feed unavailable", }; } } async function getFeed(source: FeedSource, section: SectionId, force: boolean): Promise { const cacheKey = `${source.id}:${section}`; const cached = feedCache.get(cacheKey); if (!force && cached && Date.now() - cached.fetchedAt < FEED_TTL) { return { source, items: cached.items, status: "ok", fromCache: true }; } const key = `${source.id}:${section}`; const pending = inFlight.get(key); if (pending) return pending; const request = requestFeed(source, section).finally(() => inFlight.delete(key)); inFlight.set(key, request); return request; } export async function getNews( section: SectionId, region: RegionId | null, force = false, ): Promise { const sources = feedsFor(section, region); const results = await Promise.all(sources.map((source) => getFeed(source, section, force))); const seenUrls = new Set(); const seenHeadlines = new Set(); const items = results .flatMap((result) => result.items) .sort((a, b) => { const aTime = a.publishedAt ? new Date(a.publishedAt).getTime() : 0; const bTime = b.publishedAt ? new Date(b.publishedAt).getTime() : 0; return bTime - aTime; }) .filter((item) => { const url = canonicalUrl(item.url); const headline = `${item.sourceName}:${item.title.toLowerCase().replace(/\W+/g, " ").trim()}`; if (seenUrls.has(url) || seenHeadlines.has(headline)) return false; seenUrls.add(url); seenHeadlines.add(headline); return true; }) .slice(0, MAX_ITEMS_PER_SECTION); return { section, region, items, sources: results.map((result) => ({ id: result.source.id, name: result.source.name, status: result.status, itemCount: result.items.length, ...(result.message ? { message: result.message } : {}), })), checkedAt: new Date().toISOString(), fromCache: results.length > 0 && results.every((result) => result.fromCache), }; } ================================================================================ FILE: api/queries/connection.ts ================================================================================ import { drizzle } from "drizzle-orm/mysql2";
